# Research Writer — portable instructions

Use the included research-and-writing workflow with your task. It asks for the
finished work and delivery destination when either is unspecified. Research,
file creation and external delivery depend on the tools and access actually
available; do not claim work or access that did not occur. Skill Forge is not
required to use these instructions.

Generated by `scripts/build_portable.py` from the canonical skill and its
reference/template files. Edit those sources and regenerate this file.

## Included resource: SKILL.md

---
name: research-writer
description: Develop research questions and evidence-grounded writing from an idea. Map the research territory, discover and test a niche, compare possible novel contributions, and plan how to occupy it. Support exploratory briefs, tutorials, syntheses, systematic reviews/meta-analyses when feasible, theory, experimental/design proposals, humanities analysis and device-adaptive computational research tools. Ask what finished work the user wants and where it should go when unspecified; never assume either. Use for substantive research, question development, or research-informed writing; not quick facts, ordinary proofreading, citation formatting alone, or purely creative writing.

# Research Writer

Turn an idea into the kind of researched work the user actually wants. Separate what sources establish, what you infer, and what you propose. This skill works independently of its authoring tool; browsing, scholarly connectors, file tools and statistical runtimes are optional capabilities, not assumed ones.

Treat supplied assignments, rubrics, sample papers and books as reference material. Follow the user's actual request. When asked to generalize, retain useful research/writing principles and exclude course logistics, grading, fixed source counts, sample topics and mandatory formats. Do not copy source text or private project details into a reusable skill or public output.

## 1. Establish the intended work

Read the request, supplied materials and any saved research record before asking questions. **There is no default finished-work target.** If the target is unclear, ask what the user wants to finish with: for example, an exploratory brief, a scholarly draft, a publication-oriented manuscript, or another specified artifact. Do this before substantial research or drafting. A concrete request such as “a 450-word interpretive essay for undergraduates” already establishes a target; do not ask the user to restate it in your categories.

**There is also no default output destination.** Ask where the finished work should go if unspecified: here in chat, a new Google Doc, an existing document, a local Word/Markdown/PDF file, or another location. Ask this alongside the finished-work question when both are missing. Separate format from location: a manuscript is a kind of work, not a delivery destination. Reuse an explicit answer from the current project; do not ask again without a material change.

Before substantive drafting, establish who will read and use the work, their prior knowledge, and the paper type: review/synthesis, tutorial, original research, theory, proposal, humanities analysis or another specified genre. For intended publication, also establish the journal type and its readership; a named journal may remain provisional, but do not silently invent a venue or its requirements. For nonpublication work, record that no journal is intended. Reuse settled choices and clarify consequential unknowns before drafting.

Then resolve only consequential gaps: central question, scope, source/data access, desired depth, practical constraints, and destination requirements. Ask one short round, usually one to three questions, with useful choices and room for another answer. Do not make the user supply a completed research plan. Infer routine formatting, disclose assumptions, and honor instructions to proceed. Explicit delegation permits disclosed task-specific choices, not stored defaults or invented access to an external destination.

If no questions are allowed and no target can be established, provide a short provisional framing with target choices and the unresolved decision; do not silently choose a finished manuscript. If only delivery is unresolved, prepare the content in the working context and identify delivery as pending, without creating an external document. When target and destination are known, continue authorized work without requiring approval of every stage. For external documents, use [delivery guidance](#resource-references-delivery-md).

Keep a brief covering the intended artifact, question, audience, constraints, selected methods, evidence gaps and completion criteria. For sustained work, use [the research record](#resource-templates-research-record-md) in a task-specific location outside this installed skill. Never store a user's project or preferences in this reusable package.

## 2. Develop the question and research contribution

When an idea or question is still developing, use [question development](#resource-references-question-development-md) before committing to a thesis or method. Research should help formulate the question, not merely collect support for a chosen answer. Expand the conceptual map first, then develop an encompassing but answerable main question and connected subquestions.

Collaborate around three moves, explaining the terms in plain language: **territory**—the conversation and established knowledge; **niche**—a supported unresolved issue, tension or limitation; **occupying the niche**—what this work could contribute and how. Ask about the user's interests and intended contribution when these are unsettled, but help discover the niche through research rather than requiring the user to know it already.

Use orientation searches, inspected sources, competing accounts and closest prior work to propose a small set of distinct question/contribution options. Compare their evidence, significance, feasibility and novelty uncertainty. Identify the single closest published neighbor by substantive overlap where the evidence supports one, inspect what it did and did not establish, and compare additional close works when needed. Summarize the supported increment in one sentence, then run a duplication challenge; that sentence is a positioning claim, not proof of a gap. Test whether the proposed gap matters, fits the intended readers/venue, and can be filled with available evidence and resources. Revise, justify replication or stop when prior work already occupies it; never guarantee publishability. Link each option to source IDs and distinguish source claims from your proposed inference. Novelty may lie in explanation, interpretation, synthesis, method, a meaningful boundary or a justified replication; an empty topic or new label is not enough.

Ask one to three consequential questions at the appropriate stage and preserve the user's choice. Do useful research between rounds. Do not reopen a clear, bounded writing request or turn every task into an interview. Respect no-question and supplied-sources-only instructions; infer a provisional map from available evidence and state its limits.

## 3. Check the premises and select methods

Restate the desired outcome separately from the proposed explanation or implementation. Check ambiguous terminology, hidden assumptions and conflicts with established evidence. Correct consequential premises with sources before building on them. Distinguish an impossible premise, an untested claim and a difficult engineering goal; do not dismiss an idea merely because it is unfamiliar.

For a novel proposal, build a short dependency map: what must be true, what has been demonstrated, what transfers only by analogy, and what remains speculative. Connect evidence synthesis to proposal choices. Do not equate “not found in this search” with novelty or impossibility.

Choose the applicable route in [writing modes](#resource-references-modes-md). Hybrid work may need several routes, such as synthesis → theory → experimental proposal. Keep their different evidence standards visible. A systematic review, meta-analysis, qualitative synthesis and humanities interpretation are not interchangeable labels. Descriptive or exploratory work can have a clear question without a directional hypothesis; choose the method for the question rather than force the question into a familiar tool.

## Optional research and computation tools

For substantive literature organization or computational research, use [the computational workflow](#resource-references-computational-research-md) and the original runnable tools in [evidence_tools.py](#resource-scripts-evidence-tools-py) and [research_tools.py](#resource-scripts-research-tools-py). These are optional; do not turn a humanities essay or literature-only request into a molecular experiment. Read [tool usage](#resource-references-tool-usage-md) for exact commands. A user-owned runtime profile outside the skill (for example `~/.local/share/research-writer/runtime.json`) can identify that device's interpreter; project-specific choices take precedence. Before relying on a tool, inspect [the capability catalog](#resource-references-tool-catalog-json), run the local inventory and read [device-profile guidance](#resource-references-device-profiles-md). Adapt to the user's actual OS, architecture, Python environments, CPU/GPU and local/remote setup. A profile is configuration data, not permission, installed software, a successful probe or authorization for remote jobs. Do not assume a particular laptop/GPU, read secrets, or automatically download weights, transmit private sequences or start paid/background work.

The original utilities support project records, DOI/arXiv/PMID normalization, bounded public metadata lookup, context-and-artifact-linked evidence checks, FASTA inspection, RDKit descriptors/conformers and an OpenMM analytic smoke test when the dependencies are present. Keep source-audited, dependency-present, smoke-tested and scientifically validated states separate. Heavy engines are optional preparation routes, not bundled models or guaranteed execution. Code, weights, datasets, output and services may have different terms. Selected optional execution routes are now available through [molecular conversion](#resource-references-molecular-conversion-md) and [small protein embeddings](#resource-references-protein-embeddings-md). Use the actual configured executable/interpreter and verified local assets; only explicit model preparation may download the pinned small checkpoint. Keep structural identity checks and representation outputs separate from docking, folding, biological function and benchmark validation. Larger engines remain preparation routes until separately provisioned and tested. For an explicitly selected remote job, use [bounded remote execution](#resource-references-remote-execution-md): inspect the actual host, prepare only approved files, submit once within stated limits, reconcile status, verify collected artifacts and cancel through the owning controller. A saved profile or uncertain reply never authorizes submission or resubmission. Preserve known-host verification, host/user identity, failed/partial results and resource limits. Default local research requires no remote connection. For a selected compatible Linux engine, [Linux isolation and recovery](#resource-references-linux-isolation-md) adds enforced container limits and recovery by immutable container identity. Choose it explicitly, inspect the existing engine/image and resource budget, collect within the finite retention window, and never resubmit an uncertain job. Missing Linux capabilities must fail closed; legacy SSH remains trusted-task only. When durable saving is requested or agreed for a long task, use [durable checkpoints](#resource-references-durable-checkpoints-md) for approved local artifacts or an explicitly opted-in cooperative Linux application. Choose the store path and capacity, commit verified bytes before relying on recovery, and restore into a new location. Checkpoints preserve files and declared application state; they do not capture process memory, authorize execution, prove scientific consistency or automatically resume a job. Reconcile the original execution and current permissions before any explicitly prepared continuation. When an off-device backup is requested, use [verified SSH backups](#resource-references-off-device-backups-md): select the destination and byte budget explicitly, preserve the source, reconcile an uncertain upload by immutable identity, and verify readback and restored bytes before relying on recovery. A loopback test or hostname cannot establish a separate failure domain. Backup contents and saved notes never authorize execution. For offline installation and environment checks, see [testing guidance](#resource-references-testing-md). For bounded Crossref/Europe PMC discovery, correction metadata and accessible open full texts, use [literature retrieval](#resource-references-literature-retrieval-md). For a claim-to-passage review record, use [source support](#resource-references-source-support-md). Search receipts establish retrieval coverage; a checked support record establishes artifact/version/passage consistency and records the reviewer's judgment, not automatic semantic proof. Inspect the primary text and contrary evidence before assigning support, contradiction or uncertainty. Keep metadata, abstracts, retrieved full text and reviewed evidence distinct. For broader bounded metadata/citation leads and version-pinned arXiv HTML, use [deeper retrieval](#resource-references-deeper-retrieval-md). For multiple consequential claims or conflicting sources, use [claim audit](#resource-references-claim-audit-md): declare relevant claim/source pairs, inspect scope and contrary versions, retain partial/contradictory/uncertain judgments, and check missing reviews without treating a valid inventory as drafting clearance. Match access requirements to each claim; metadata, provider flags and extraction hashes never substitute for reading.

For an actual computational experiment, record the hypothesis, baseline/comparators, data/splits, parameters/seeds, metrics/units, budget and stop rule before execution where possible. Preserve every failed, negative and amended run, command/environment and input/output hashes; bound repair/refine/pivot attempts to the authorized budget. Do not silently select the best trial as confirmatory evidence or modify this installed skill/global memory automatically. Generate result tables from recorded artifacts and match each number to its run/condition/dataset/metric/unit/seed/statistic. A number appearing elsewhere or a verified DOI alone does not support a claim. Review semantic usefulness separately from arithmetic/provenance.

Protein structures, sequence scores, docking, generated molecules and simulations are model outputs. Confidence is not measured affinity; prediction, a converged optimizer or a stable trajectory does not prove biological function. Noncanonical chemistry needs compatible representations, components, topology and parameters in the selected engines. Unsupported residues/cofactors must remain explicit rather than silently mapped to X/canonical residues. Computational outputs require appropriate controls, uncertainty and independent experimental evidence before stronger claims.

## 4. Research with an auditable trail

Follow [research and source handling](#resource-references-research-and-sources-md). Match effort to the agreed artifact. Define a bounded search plan and stopping rule; record coverage gaps instead of suggesting an unbounded search is exhaustive. If the user gives no effort budget, state a reasonable search scope for this task and expand it only when unresolved questions justify it.

Use supplied evidence and actually available research tools. Read underlying material before relying on it; snippets and generated summaries are leads. Check both the identity of a source and whether its inspected content supports the particular claim. Look for contrary findings, alternate mechanisms, relevant reviews and primary evidence. Preserve the difference between independent studies and repeated reports of the same study.

For deep research, iterate from field orientation to comparison of the closest precedents, then challenge the proposed niche and resolve the most consequential evidence gaps. Follow terminology across adjacent fields and citation trails when useful. Save what each research pass changed about the question. Depth means resolving important uncertainties and engaging conflicting evidence, not accumulating a fixed number of sources or announcing an exhaustive search.

If browsing, full texts, data or statistical execution are unavailable, say what the limitation prevents and deliver the best warranted artifact: a bounded synthesis, source-backed section, search protocol, feasibility assessment or proposal. Never fabricate access, searches, citations, study counts, analysis or completed work. Respect an instruction to use only supplied materials.

## 5. Develop the argument and write

**Before the first substantive draft and after a material change or resumed project, complete [the prewriting gate](#resource-references-prewriting-md): establish readers/genre/venue context, review the actual search record, perform warranted search renewal and reconcile it with prior evidence, then test the nearest-neighbor contribution and duplication claim.** Record the dated search scope, actual update or reason no repeat is needed, and what changed. A just-completed adequate search need not be repeated identically. A supplied-only boundary governs the check; blocked access is not a completed search. Resolve material unknowns or explicitly scope the work before drafting; do not present an unrenewed review or untested novelty claim as verified.

Organize around the question and audience, not the order sources were found. Build a claim-to-evidence map, an outline and the requested draft. Keep internal scaffolding out of a short finished artifact unless requested. A broad task should produce substantive work, not stop at an outline unless that is the target or an unresolved dependency prevents honest completion.

For publication-oriented work or a submission-readiness handoff, use [publication preparation](#resource-references-publication-workflow-md). Keep every aim aligned with its evidence, method, actual result and interpretation; make missing preparation facts explicit without inventing declarations. In a readiness handoff, account for author approval, each of data/code/materials availability, relevant ethics versus unknown institutional claims, and venue-dependent cover material; a nonapplicable field needs a reason rather than silent omission. A theory, review or humanities manuscript keeps its own genre.

Use [argument and synthesis guidance](#resource-references-argument-and-synthesis-md) to connect claims, evidence and warrants, represent disagreements fairly and align question, contribution and method. Adapt territory → niche → contribution to the introduction when useful; it is a reasoning aid, not a universal heading scheme or a license to invent findings.

Distinguish established findings, contextual background, interpretation, hypotheses and proposals in the prose. Include important counterevidence and competing interpretations. State the actual basis for each conclusion. Do not force scientific paper headings onto humanities writing or a proposal. Match journal or disciplinary requirements only after checking the relevant current instructions and actual eligibility.

Revise for argument, support, competing accounts and alignment with the writing brief before proofreading. Use clear terminology suited to readers, purposeful transitions, precise numbers/units, and hedging that matches uncertainty. Choose active or passive voice for clarity and disciplinary purpose; neither is a universal rule. Use verified references in the requested style. Keep citations close to the claims they support. Do not cite a real paper for an assertion it does not support. Mark necessary unresolved claims visibly in a working draft; remove or qualify them before calling the draft verified. Speculative proposals may be valuable while remaining explicitly speculative.

## 6. Audit and deliver

Check the finished work against the brief and the selected mode's gate. Verify quotations and locators, consequential factual claims, references, numerical results and all assertions of performed work. Check that causal language, certainty and scope match the evidence. Confirm that limitations are specific and that conclusions survive the strongest contrary evidence considered.

Revisit the positioning after research: is the niche still supported, does the actual method answer the question, and does the claimed contribution differ meaningfully from the closest prior work? Report novelty only within the search and access actually achieved. A well-supported continuation, synthesis or reinterpretation can be worthwhile without a first-ever claim.

Deliver the requested artifact to the agreed destination, plus a proportionate account of evidence coverage, remaining gaps and next decisions. Verify the actual delivered content and return its usable link or file when relevant. For substantial research, retain a source ledger and search/methods record alongside the draft. Provide separate files when useful and supported; otherwise provide clearly separated text. Never claim publication readiness, exhaustive coverage, independent human review or successful experimental validation merely because the prose is polished.

If work is interrupted, save what is complete, what is unverified, the last search performed and the next action. Do not invent persistent memory, automatic follow-ups or research completed between turns.

<a id="resource-references-argument-and-synthesis-md"></a>

## Included resource: references/argument-and-synthesis.md

# Build an argument from sources

## Give sources distinct jobs

Keep three layers separate: what a source actually says, your appraisal of its evidence and limits, and how you think it changes this project. For substantial work, record source roles and relationships in a lightweight matrix. An annotated bibliography can support discovery or be a requested deliverable; it is not automatically required for every task.

Compare agreements, contradictions, definitions, methods and contexts across sources. Write synthesis around those relationships and the research question, rather than stringing together annotations. Do not treat a sample bibliography's citations or summaries as verified merely because the document looks complete. Verify relied-on references and underlying claims through the normal source workflow.

## Connect claim, evidence and warrant

For each important claim identify the evidence and the inferential bridge explaining why it supports that claim. Check assumptions, competing explanations and appropriate qualifications. A quotation or citation does not supply the reasoning automatically. Distinguish observations, participants' reports, source authors' interpretations and your conclusions. Association, narrative plausibility and analogy do not establish causation.

Represent the strongest relevant counterargument fairly and explain what it would change. Avoid cherry-picking, false binaries, overgeneralization, circular definitions, irrelevant authority and dismissing a source because its conclusion is inconvenient. Use contrary or mixed evidence to refine scope and contribution rather than treating it as an obstacle to a predetermined thesis.

## Match inquiry to evidence and method

Distinguish reading primary sources, analyzing existing data/materials, and collecting new firsthand data. Reading an empirical paper during a literature review is different from personally conducting its experiment; a primary text can support humanities research without new human-participant recruitment.

Choose the method because it can answer the question. Observations concern observable behavior; interviews offer accounts and depth; surveys measure reported experiences or views within their sampling limits. Neither self-reports nor convenience samples automatically support population-wide behavioral or causal claims. Pilot an instrument or analytical scheme when relevant; use clear nonleading questions and explicit selection rules. Plan for analysis and writing as well as collection. Apply ethics and approval requirements to the actual work, and verify current requirements rather than inheriting a teaching example's exemption.

## Write for the reader without distorting the inquiry

Explain terms, context and stakes for the intended audience. Build credibility through accuracy, transparent limits and fair representation. Use examples, narrative or human consequences when they clarify the argument, while preserving the evidence. Persuasive force, emotional appeal, impressive citations and audience agreement do not establish validity. Reject rhetorical tactics that evade inconvenient facts or knowingly exploit fallacies. Scholarly hedging should track uncertainty, not be removed simply to make a claim sound stronger.

Structure and emphasis follow genre. A theoretical paper may need definitions, mechanisms and objections; a proposal needs aims, rationale, feasible methods and anticipated contribution; an interpretive essay needs a contestable thesis and close analysis. Include abstract, budget, timeline, presentation or a particular section scheme only when the actual artifact or outlet calls for it. Course deadlines, grading criteria, fixed counts and sample project details are never global skill defaults.

<a id="resource-references-claim-audit-md"></a>

## Included resource: references/claim-audit.md

# Audit a declared claim-to-source matrix

Use `scripts/claim_audit.py` when consequential claims depend on multiple sources,
versions or competing findings. It supplements the unchanged legacy
[source-support checker](#resource-references-source-support-md). It makes no network request.

```sh
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/claim_audit.py audit-claims --input PROJECT/claims.json --output PROJECT/claim-audit.json
```

Use a new output path. The manifest has integer `schema_version: 1` and arrays
`sources`, `claims` and `reviews`. The manifest must be a regular UTF-8 JSON file. All source paths are project-relative UTF-8
regular files confined to the manifest directory. Nonregular objects such as
named pipes are rejected before reading. The legacy integrity checker reopens
paths, so use a cooperating filesystem without concurrent source replacement;
this is not an adversarial filesystem sandbox. Retain originals and extraction
provenance; do not edit text after reviewing or hashing it. Bounds are 32 sources,
100 claims, 500 reviews, 10 MiB per manifest/source and 20 MiB aggregate sources.

Each **source** uses the legacy fields `id`, `version`, `access`, `identity_status`,
`path`, `sha256` and `locator`, plus nonempty `study_family` and
`publication_status` (`current`, `corrected`, `retracted`, `withdrawn`, `unknown`).
Access is `metadata`, `abstract` or `full_text`. Establish identity, version,
family and status from inspected evidence; these declarations are not
independently authenticated by the checker. A provider flag alone is not a
status review. Preserve correction notices and replaced versions; do not count
them as new independent experiments.

Each **claim** has `id`, exact `text`, nonempty unique `required_source_ids` and
`required_access` (`abstract` or `full_text`). Declare the relevant source set
before selecting favorable passages. This is an explicit scope, not proof that
all claims or contrary sources have been discovered. Amendments to the inventory
need a recorded reason; deleting difficult sources is not resolution.

Each **review** has legacy `id`, `claim`, `source_id`, `source_version`,
`source_sha256`, `passage`, `start`, `end`, `locator`, `judgment`, `rationale`,
`reviewer`, timezone-bearing `reviewed_at`, plus `claim_id`. Its claim text must
exactly match the declared claim. Offsets count decoded Unicode characters,
start inclusive/end exclusive. Judgment is `supports`, `partial`, `contradicts`
or `uncertain`. Preserve multiple passages/judgments, including additional
contrary reviews beyond the required minimum. The reviewer's rationale must
come from actual reading, not keyword overlap or the integrity check.

Read enough surrounding methods, results and limitations to judge the claim's
population, outcome, time point and causal scope. A passage supporting a narrower
association is partial support for a broader causal claim. Consider correction
and publication status before relying on an earlier result. Look for contrary
evidence and distinguish unavailable methods from negative findings. For a
contested claim, have a reviewer separate from drafting inspect the source;
disclose agent-based/nonblind review and unresolved domain-expertise limits.

The result separates record integrity, coverage of declared claim/source pairs,
recorded judgments, declared family counts and flags needing review. Missing
required reviews yield `incomplete`; unsafe/stale/inconsistent records yield
`failed`; an empty claim inventory is `not_checked`. Only `records_valid` exits
zero. A valid contradiction record may still be `records_valid`: that means the
record is consistent, not that the claim is supported. Partial/contradictory/
uncertain judgments and adverse/unknown status stay visible for revision.

Every report keeps `semantic_entailment: not_automatically_verified` and
`drafting_clearance: not_granted`. Narrow, qualify, omit or investigate claims
through actual evidence review before drafting them as established. A valid
inventory cannot detect undeclared claims, dishonest judgments, undiscovered
corrections or false family declarations, and cannot certify scientific truth.

<a id="resource-references-computational-research-md"></a>

## Included resource: references/computational-research.md

# Computational research and evidence records

First settle the finished work and destination. An empirical paper needs executed evidence; a proposal needs an executable design clearly labelled as proposed. A literature review does not require invented experiments. Keep records in the user's selected project and preserve its existing layout.

## Establish a project

Run from the skill directory, substituting the selected project path:

```sh
python3 scripts/evidence_tools.py init-project --directory /path/to/project
python3 scripts/research_tools.py doctor --output /path/to/project/doctor.json
python3 scripts/research_tools.py profile --output /path/to/project/device.json
python3 scripts/research_tools.py plan --tool researchclaw --profile /path/to/project/device.json --input /path/to/project/research-project.json --output /path/to/project/execution-plan.json
```

The initializer creates only a missing `research-project.json`; `target` and `destination` start as null and must be settled with the user. Existing files remain unchanged. Record scope, decisions, tool capabilities, budgets and paths to sources, data, scripts, runs and drafts. Keep original data separate from derived outputs. Retain dataset versions, licenses and acquisition dates. Never scan credentials or create global memory. Project lessons are reviewable notes, not automatic changes to this skill or other projects.

Edit the generated device profile and select a tool as documented in the [main CLI guide](#resource-references-tool-usage-md) to choose a feasible CPU/GPU plan. The example plan records a possible ResearchClaw setup; it does not execute it. Device availability is not proof that a method or package works on it. Pilot with small data, low memory and short runtime before scaling. Record any CPU fallback, reduced precision, smaller model/data or changed method; assess how it changes the question. No silent simulated measurements.

## Search and normalize sources

```sh
python3 scripts/evidence_tools.py search --query "cross validation small samples" --limit 5 --output /path/to/project/search.json
python3 scripts/evidence_tools.py lookup-doi --doi 10.1038/nrn3241 --output /path/to/project/identity.json
python3 scripts/evidence_tools.py bibliography --input /path/to/project/bibliography-input.json --output /path/to/project/bibliography-normalized.json
```

These network commands use the public Crossref API with a ten-second timeout and bounded responses. Queries/DOIs leave the computer; do not include confidential text. They need no key or model weights. Results are **metadata only**. Status distinguishes success, empty, unavailable and failed; errors are not evidence that a paper does not exist. DOI lookup checks returned identity. Read the actual source before assessing support. Record metadata, abstract or full-text access honestly, with a URL and page/section/table/passage locator. Source identity and access do not establish claim entailment, peer review or methodological quality.

Bibliography input is a JSON list of objects using `doi`, `arxiv` and/or `pmid`; each can also be a list of variants. Other fields are retained. Shared normalized identities merge records; original records, arXiv versions and provenance remain visible. Invalid identifiers and conflicting identities/metadata require review. Do not fabricate missing metadata.

## Run bounded experiments

Before execution, record hypotheses, comparison conditions, baselines, data/splits, metrics/units, seeds, uncertainty estimates, resource limits and a stop rule. Separate exploratory changes from confirmatory plans. Pilot first; diagnose failure from logs; refine implementation within a fixed attempt budget or pivot the question with a recorded reason. Preserve every attempt, failed run, negative finding and protocol amendment. Report selection criteria when highlighting a best run. A successful exit and matching numbers do not establish scientific usefulness: inspect leakage, confounding, identical ablations and whether measurements answer the question.

Human review belongs at direction selection, consequential design changes and claim interpretation. Run critique serially when delegation is unavailable or unauthorized. Do not install dependencies, start cloud jobs, upload private data or publish merely because a workflow suggests them.

## Verify recorded evidence

```sh
python3 scripts/evidence_tools.py verify-evidence --input /path/to/project/evidence.json --output /path/to/project/integrity-report.json
```

Manifest arrays are `artifacts`, `measurements`, `claims`, `sources` and `source_claims`; absent arrays are empty. Every record requires a unique string `id` within its array. Artifacts have a project-relative CSV `path` and actual `sha256`; traversal and escaping symlinks fail. CSV headers include `run,condition,dataset,metric,unit,seed,statistic,value`. Measurement records supply those same fields plus `artifact_id` and `row` (first CSV data row is 1). Context values are nonempty strings, including seeds such as `"42"` or `"not_applicable"`; values are finite decimal numbers or decimal strings. Claims contain the same context/value and link through `evidence_id`. Exact Decimal comparison uses no tolerance or global number whitelist.

Sources declare `access`, `locator` and `identity_status` (`verified` or `identity_verified` for cited sources). Source claims link `source_id`, declare `required_access` and supply their own `locator`. Metadata cannot satisfy full-text access. Passing reports check recorded integrity, **not semantic entailment**. With no numeric or source claims, status is `not_checked`, with reason `no_claims_to_check`, and exit 2. Reports are deterministic, list errors and exit 2 on failed checks; inputs are limited to 10 MiB. Existing outputs require explicit `--force` to replace. Generate final tables from the checked records, then assess meaning and source support before drafting.

Design references: [AutoResearchClaw](https://github.com/aiming-lab/AutoResearchClaw), its [paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20025v2), and [Beeblio](https://github.com/alharkan7/beeblio). Their performance claims are not reproduced here. This toolkit is original; Beeblio's noncommercial code is not bundled.

## Included resource: references/deeper-retrieval.md

# Broader discovery and version-pinned full text

The optional `scripts/discovery_tools.py` uses Python 3.10+ standard library.
Run it explicitly in a user-owned project. Search terms and requested identifiers
are sent to the selected public service; honor supplied-only and confidentiality
boundaries. No API key, account registration or paid fallback is provided.

```sh
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/discovery_tools.py search --query "retrieval augmented generation" --limit 5 --output PROJECT/discovery.json
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/discovery_tools.py work --work-id W3027879771 --output PROJECT/work.json
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/discovery_tools.py arxiv-fulltext --arxiv-id 2005.11401v4 --text-output PROJECT/article.txt --output PROJECT/article.json
```

`work` also accepts `--doi DOI` instead of `--work-id`. IDs are validated and the
returned work/DOI must match the request. Search is one OpenAlex page, at most
20 records. A returned hit count is provider metadata, not screened evidence;
a small first page is never fieldwide coverage. Reference IDs are citation-trail
leads, not papers retrieved or read. Keep multiple reports/versions and study
families distinct. Existing Crossref/Europe PMC routes remain available through
[literature retrieval](#resource-references-literature-retrieval-md).

Anonymous OpenAlex requests use a shared-IP free quota. Rate limits and service
availability can change; failures, quota exhaustion and missing records remain
visible. This tool does not acquire a key, enable cursor paging or purchase more
capacity. Provider OA links, license fields and `is_retracted` flags do not prove
full-text access, article reuse rights or certified publication status. Check
relevant primary versions and notices before relying on affected findings.

The arXiv command requires an explicit version, including for legacy IDs. It
fetches only the fixed official HTML route, independently of the Atom API.
A recognized front identity/version signal and a substantive article body are
required; a matching ID in a bibliography, asset or footer is insufficient.
Wrong versions, abstract/error pages and unavailable conversions are not full
text. No silent latest-version substitution, mirror, PDF fallback, asset fetch
or script execution occurs. Each response is capped at 10 MiB and uses a ten-second socket timeout; this is
not a hard end-to-end wall-time deadline. Existing output paths are preserved
before any network request. The parser requires at least 80 characters and
12 words of prose outside abstracts/bibliographies; very short or differently
structured documents may be unavailable through this conservative route.

Keep arXiv operations sequential and at least three seconds apart across all
processes and machines under your control. The utility's local serialization
cannot coordinate other programs/devices; do not run parallel crawls. The HTML
route does not establish availability for every paper. Mathematical notation,
tables, images, reading order and conversion errors may require inspection of
the original; extracted text is a separate versioned artifact.

Reports retain request/time/HTTP and raw-byte/extraction hashes, pinned identity,
access and locators. Successful retrieval keeps reading and semantic review
unperformed. Store source artifacts in the project, inspect the relevant text
and contrary evidence, then use [claim audit](#resource-references-claim-audit-md) for a declared
claim inventory. A content hash pins bytes, not the truth of their claims.
Unknown article rights remain unknown; downloaded paper text is not part of
this MIT skill and must not be redistributed automatically.

Official references inspected for this route: [OpenAlex API](https://help.openalex.org/api/),
[authentication](https://help.openalex.org/api/authentication/),
[arXiv API terms](https://info.arxiv.org/help/api/tou.html), and
[HTML guidance](https://info.arxiv.org/about/accessible_HTML.html).

<a id="resource-references-delivery-md"></a>

## Included resource: references/delivery.md

# Deliver where the user requested

Establish the destination during intake. Chat, a new document, an existing document and a downloadable file are distinct choices. Do not treat the conversational interface as an implied final destination when none was specified. Reuse a clear destination already supplied for the current work; avoid repeated confirmation.

## Resolve the concrete target

- **Chat:** provide the finished work in the conversation, with citations and any necessary supporting material.
- **New Google Doc or another connected document:** verify that the integration is available and use its supported creation workflow. A request to create the document authorizes that creation; do not add a blanket final approval step. Choose a sensible title unless the user specified one.
- **Existing document:** obtain or resolve the exact document and intended edit scope. Inspect it before editing, preserve unrelated content, and ask only if the target or replace/append scope is materially ambiguous. A link alone does not authorize replacing the entire document.
- **Local file:** establish the requested format and use the task workspace unless the user specified another permitted location. Use available document-authoring skills or tools when appropriate, and inspect the rendered result for formats whose layout matters.
- **Other service or destination:** use supported capabilities and check that the actual destination is reachable. Never invent a successful upload or a document URL.

Use the relevant installed destination-specific skill or connector instructions when available; the core workflow does not depend on any one tool name. If connection or permission is missing, complete the research and prepare the artifact you can produce, then explain the specific obstacle and ask for the missing access or an alternate destination. Do not silently substitute a local file for a requested Google Doc or claim delivery merely because a draft exists.

## Verify and report

Check that the saved document contains the intended text, references, headings and tables, and that the returned link identifies the actual artifact. Keep private research records out of a public document unless requested. Preserve the requested access level; creating a document is not authorization to publish it publicly, change sharing permissions or send it to someone.

When a write or upload fails, report the failure and preserve the draft. Check destination state before retrying if duplicate creation is possible. Distinguish a saved draft, a delivered artifact and publication. End with the actual output or link and any unfinished delivery step.

<a id="resource-references-device-profiles-md"></a>

## Included resource: references/device-profiles.md

# Device profiles and capability checks

The catalog records source-audited interfaces, not installed tools. Discover the actual interpreter, package versions, command paths and platform on the machine that will run the work. Keep source revision, detected dependency presence, optional probe results, smoke results and scientific validation as separate evidence. A declared CUDA/MPS/remote setting is not a successful capability probe.

From the skill directory, replace `PROJECT` with the selected project/configuration directory outside the installed skill:

```sh
python scripts/research_tools.py profile --output PROJECT/device-profile.json
python scripts/research_tools.py doctor --output PROJECT/doctor.json
python scripts/research_tools.py doctor --profile PROJECT/device-profile.json --output PROJECT/doctor.json
python scripts/research_tools.py doctor --profile PROJECT/device-profile.json --probe-gpu --output PROJECT/doctor-gpu.json
python scripts/research_tools.py plan --tool boltz --input PROJECT/complex.yaml --profile PROJECT/device-profile.json --output PROJECT/boltz-plan.json
```

`plan` prepares a reviewable recipe; it does not execute the engine, install dependencies, download weights, generate MSAs or submit sequences. Check its unresolved prerequisites and limitations before proceeding. The helper also provides bounded local `fasta`, `rdkit-descriptors`, `rdkit-conformer` and `openmm-smoke` operations; use the helper's help for their exact arguments. A conformer or artificial OpenMM energy check is not a validated research study.

Keep the profile in a user-owned research project/configuration directory **outside the installed skill**. Reuse only isolated runtime paths supplied in that user's configuration. Do not embed the skill author's machine paths or copy credentials into profiles, notes or reports.

Profile fields:

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| `schema_version` | `1` |
| `mode` | `"local"` or `"remote"` |
| `python` | Optional interpreter path string |
| `remote_host` | Optional remote-host string; no passwords/tokens |
| `accelerator` | `"auto"`, `"cpu"`, `"cuda"`, `"mps"`, `"rocm"` or `"unknown"` |
| `executables` | Object mapping catalog engine IDs to user-supplied executable/script paths |
| `model_dirs` | Object mapping catalog engine IDs to user-supplied model directories |
| `notes` | String with nonsecret compatibility context |

A minimal declaration is:

```json
{
  "schema_version": 1,
  "mode": "local",
  "accelerator": "auto",
  "executables": {},
  "model_dirs": {},
  "notes": ""
}
```

A profile is data and grants **no authority** to install, execute remotely, spend money, upload inputs or accept terms. Default doctor checks describe current-interpreter packages/platform and command paths. They do not establish that a different declared interpreter contains those packages. Invoke the helper using the intended environment's interpreter to examine that environment. GPU probing is an explicit extra check; detected hardware still needs a compatible engine build/kernel and a task-sized acceptance run. Remote profiles remain **unprobed**: doctor does not SSH, authenticate or execute there. Obtain remote evidence only through a separately authorized workflow.

Use separate environments where dependencies conflict. In particular, Meta `fair-esm` and Biohub `esm` share the `esm` import namespace and need separate environments. DeepChem, DiffDock and REINVENT4 have materially different Python/Torch/numpy requirements. Do not install every catalog package into one environment.

CUDA, ROCm and Apple MPS are different backends; one detected accelerator does not imply support by every engine. CPU fallback is useful only where the selected interface/build supports it and the runtime is acceptable. Check Windows/WSL, macOS architecture and Linux support per engine; no universal portability is promised. AF3 source exposes CPU/MPS paths beyond older installation prose, while GNINA's runtime CPU flag does not remove its source-build CUDA dependency. OpenMM's small diagnostic should select Reference explicitly; real simulations need parameters and a protocol.

Before heavy work, verify exact runtime/model versions and hashes, input chemistry and provenance, compatible topology/tokenization/parameters, licenses and service terms, model/database/cache/temp storage, RAM/VRAM, elapsed-time/output limits and spending boundaries. Missing prerequisites remain explicit. Never add automatic API fallback, checkpoint/dataset download, MSA generation, sequence/structure upload or all-package installation. Preserve failed runs and report what was actually measured.


## Executable adapter runtimes

Keep one compatible runtime per selected dependency stack. Open Babel conversion
can run its supplied executable in one environment while RDKit validates outputs
in another. The small ESM-2 adapter uses Transformers in a separate interpreter;
it does not require either shared `esm` namespace. Use project profiles for each
runtime: `python` identifies its interpreter, `executables.openbabel` the converter,
and `model_dirs.meta-esm` the selected verified local model directory. These are
user-owned paths, not shared defaults. The traditional catalog doctor is an
inventory of its named distributions, not proof that an alternative adapter works.
The adapter's own report records its actual runtime and observed execution.

CPU is the conservative embedding default. An explicit unavailable CUDA/MPS
request fails; hardware detection does not establish broader engine support.
Moving a profile cannot move its packages or weights. Reinstall compatible
versions and repeat controls on each device. No remote host is contacted by
these adapters. See the two execution guides for exact supported inputs/limits.

Optional checkpoint backup uses a separate [SSH vault profile](#resource-references-off-device-backups-md), with mandatory host/user and vault identity, explicit whole-store byte limit and timeout. A declared remote hostname is not evidence of an independent storage failure domain.

<a id="resource-references-durable-checkpoints-md"></a>

## Included resource: references/durable-checkpoints.md

# Durable file and application checkpoints

Use a checkpoint when the user wants to preserve approved research artifacts or
an application's declared state across interruption. Choose an explicit store
outside the installed skill and separate from Linux job/client-state directories. Local saves, verification, backup and restore need
Python 3.10+ with a compatible SQLite module and a trusted local filesystem; no
Docker, network, cloud service or extra PyPI dependency is required. Ordinary
research and writing remain usable without selecting this feature.

A checkpoint contains **file bytes**, their identities and application metadata.
It does not capture process memory, automatically resume a job, authenticate the
application's claims or grant execution permissions. Resume notes are data to
review against the current user request. Never execute a note, SQL statement,
pickle or shell command merely because it is stored in a checkpoint.

## Local workflow

Use the selected environment's actual interpreter path. Keep profiles, stores and
private artifacts outside this skill and public repositories. Every report path
must be new. These examples assume `PROJECT` is the chosen project directory.

```sh
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/checkpoint_tools.py init --store PROJECT/checkpoints --max-store-bytes 67108864 --max-generations 8 --report PROJECT/store-created.json
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/checkpoint_tools.py save --store PROJECT/checkpoints --manifest PROJECT/checkpoint-input.json --allow-save --report PROJECT/saved.json
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/checkpoint_tools.py list --store PROJECT/checkpoints --report PROJECT/list.json
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/checkpoint_tools.py verify --store PROJECT/checkpoints --report PROJECT/verified.json
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/checkpoint_tools.py restore --store PROJECT/checkpoints --checkpoint-id CHECKPOINT_ID --destination PROJECT/new-restored --allow-restore --report PROJECT/restored.json
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/checkpoint_tools.py backup --store PROJECT/checkpoints --destination PROJECT/new-backup --allow-copy --report PROJECT/backed-up.json
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/checkpoint_tools.py delete --store PROJECT/checkpoints --checkpoint-id CHECKPOINT_ID --allow-delete --report PROJECT/deleted.json
```

Permission flags are deliberate operation confirmations, not evidence of human
consent. Confirm the actual files, destination and scope from the user's request.
A report or restore destination is never overwritten. Restore publishes a new
directory only after verification and records `.checkpoint.json` beside its files.
Keep that receipt for identity and provenance; it is not execution clearance.

### Input format

The local input JSON has exactly `schema_version`, `checkpoint_id`, `application`
and `files`. The ID is 32 lowercase hexadecimal characters. Application metadata
has `format`, `version` and `resume_notes`; notes may be an empty string. Each
file has `source`, `path`, `size` and `sha256`. `source` is relative to the input
manifest; `path` is its restored POSIX relative name. Byte size and hash must match
the actual inspected file. A valid zero-byte file is different from a missing file.

For a self-contained synthetic example, save and run this Python snippet in the
selected project. The format also supports real approved drafts, source ledgers,
search receipts, extracted data and research records. A cloud-document URL alone
is not its saved content; export authorized bytes and retain their actual version.

```python
from pathlib import Path
import hashlib, json, uuid
raw = b'{"counter":5}\n'
Path('counter.json').write_bytes(raw)
checkpoint = {
    'schema_version': 1,
    'checkpoint_id': uuid.uuid4().hex,
    'application': {
        'format': 'synthetic-counter',
        'version': '1',
        'resume_notes': 'Example data only; choose any continuation explicitly.'
    },
    'files': [{
        'source': 'counter.json', 'path': 'state/counter.json',
        'size': len(raw), 'sha256': hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest()
    }]
}
Path('checkpoint-input.json').write_text(json.dumps(checkpoint), encoding='utf-8')
print(checkpoint['checkpoint_id'])
```

Saving exactly the same ID, content and provenance again is idempotent, including
when the first reply was lost after commit. Reusing an ID with changed contents
or provenance is refused. Choose a new ID for a new generation. Verification
checks stored bytes and metadata; it does not infer whether an application's
logical/scientific state can safely continue.

File names must be confined, normalized relative paths with no duplicate or
parent/child file conflicts. Symlinks, hardlinks, special files, traversal,
credential/configuration paths and reserved receipt/log names are refused.
Portable names also reject Windows device/alternate-stream names, illegal
characters and trailing dots/spaces. Case-folded or Unicode-normalized aliases
and their file/directory conflicts are refused instead of silently renaming them.
Snapshots contain 1–32 files, at most 8 MiB per file and 16 MiB total. JSON/RPC and
metadata are bounded. Boolean sizes, duplicate keys and nonfinite JSON fail.
Application format/version are bounded nonempty strings; notes are bounded text.

## Store, capacity and crash recovery

The store uses a private dedicated directory, a stable OS lock and
`checkpoint.sqlite`. SQLite transactions commit one whole generation at a time.
A killed writer before commit leaves the previous committed state. A client dying
after commit can retry the same checkpoint identity without creating another
copy. Do not delete lock files or journals to bypass another writer; kernel locks
release on process death and SQLite needs a hot journal to recover safely.

`--max-store-bytes` is 4–512 MiB in whole MiB; `--max-generations` is 1–128. There is
no automatic pruning. Reaching either limit refuses the new save while preserving
previous valid data. Explicit deletion removes a selected generation transactionally;
SQLite can reuse freed pages. Deletion does not promise immediate file shrinkage
or certified erasure from storage, backups or forensic copies.

The byte ceiling applies to **database pages**, not the whole directory or host.
Rollback journals, temporary backup/restore publication and destinations need
additional disk space. The helper reapplies and checks `max_page_count` on every
connection because a one-time setting does not survive reopening. If storage is
full, the transaction must fail rather than claim a completed save. Preserve the
old valid store and inspect its verification report before trying again.

The helper requests and verifies DELETE journaling and synchronous EXTRA; it also
requests fullfsync on macOS. Actual durability still depends on SQLite's VFS, OS,
filesystem, device and power behavior. Windows directory-flush behavior differs
from Unix. Process-kill recovery tests do not establish a physical power-cycle
guarantee. POSIX ownership/private modes are enforced; Windows inherits ACLs, so
choose a private parent directory. The store is not encrypted or signed against
a malicious owner. Hashes detect corruption but are not authentication.

Use a trusted **local** filesystem. Network shares, cloud-synced folders and
concurrent writers on different machines are not accepted storage configurations.
Locks on separate copied stores do not coordinate distributed execution.
The explicit backup command uses SQLite's consistent backup API and verifies the
new store. Do not raw-copy a live database while dropping its journal. Transfer a
verified backup through a currently authorized channel, then verify it on the
target device. Backup/restore itself makes no network transfer or credential copy.
A host disk failure can still destroy every checkpoint held only on that disk.

## Opt-in cooperative Linux capture

Existing schema-1 isolated jobs retain their original behavior. For a newly
prepared checkpoint-capable job, start with the complete manifest in
[Linux isolation](#resource-references-linux-isolation-md), set `schema_version` to 2 and add:

```json
{
  "checkpoint": {
    "directory": "checkpoints",
    "max_bytes": 1048576,
    "max_files": 8
  }
}
```

This is an addition to the full manifest, not a standalone job. The checkpoint
directory must not overlap declared input/output file paths. The limits are
positive integers up to 16 MiB and 32 files. Allow memory for the application,
supervisor and capture interpreter as well as encoded checkpoint bytes. A small
memory budget can kill a process and lose tmpfs before capture; do not assume
that accepting a configured ceiling guarantees a task can finish within it.

The application must write a fresh immutable generation under
`/work/checkpoints/CHECKPOINT_ID/`, then atomically publish
`/work/checkpoints/ready.json` only after its files are complete. Its exact marker
format is `schema_version:1`, `checkpoint_id`, `application` and
`files:[{path,size,sha256}]`. File paths are relative to that generation directory;
`source` is not included in the running-job marker. The marker must be a bounded
regular file. The helper checks it before and after reading the bounded regular
files, including hashes and identity/stability checks. Changing files or marker
must fail capture rather than commit a mixed generation.

Capture is read-only and leaves the task running; the application owns its
consistency protocol. Hash agreement proves the copied set matches the declaration,
not that it is a complete or logically consistent application checkpoint.
No host-writable mount, daemon socket, process-memory capture or automatic watcher
is added. A previously staged old controller cannot gain this operation by editing
its pinned source; prepare a new capable job rather than mutate an old one.

```sh
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/linux_tools.py checkpoint --state PROJECT/job-state --store PROJECT/checkpoints --allow-save --report PROJECT/captured.json
```

This operation checks the original engine/container, manifest, controller and
nonce, then records the checkpoint with Linux provenance. The host-side store
commits before the operation reports success. Large payloads are not persisted in
the client-state JSON. If the client or container dies before commit, use the last
verified checkpoint; do not fabricate a new one or resubmit the uncertain job.
If it dies after commit but before reply, inspect the store and retry only the
same identity when necessary.

## Explicit continuation

After a container stops or its temporary files disappear, the original job may
report artifacts unavailable while a previously committed checkpoint remains
valid. Verify the store, restore into a new directory and reconcile the original
execution. Confirm the target device/runtime, application format, assets, rights,
input files and current budget before preparing a new explicitly authorized job
with a fresh job ID and restored data as approved inputs. The application must
actually implement reading that state. A receipt cannot invent that capability.

The original manifest/host identities are historical provenance, not permission
to connect, transfer files or execute on another machine. No restore operation
launches a job. Keep failed/partial/negative runs and the continuation relationship
in the research record; resuming computation does not turn it into confirmatory
scientific evidence or grant drafting clearance. Recheck question/search/audience
context before resuming substantive writing under the normal prewriting gate.

## Tests and primary interfaces

`self_check.py --checkpoints` requires real local save, idempotent retry, backup,
verification and empty-file restore controls. Repository core CI adds interrupted
writer, capacity, integrity and path cases on its selected platforms. Actual Linux
capture/container-loss/new-job continuation is a separate mandatory suite. Read
the repository's durable validation report and release pull request for observed
results and unexecuted combinations.

Original helper code is MIT; no upstream SQLite/Docker/Python implementation is
redistributed. These are separately supplied runtime interfaces. References:
[SQLite PRAGMAs](https://www.sqlite.org/pragma.html),
[atomic commit](https://www.sqlite.org/atomiccommit.html),
[hot-journal recovery](https://www.sqlite.org/lockingv3.html),
[consistent backup](https://www.sqlite.org/backup.html),
[Python SQLite](https://docs.python.org/3/library/sqlite3.html),
[filesystem limits](https://www.sqlite.org/howtocorrupt.html) and
[Docker tmpfs](https://docs.docker.com/engine/storage/tmpfs/).

For a separately selected destination, [verified SSH backups](#resource-references-off-device-backups-md) add bounded whole-store transfer and readback/restore drills. The local `backup` command alone remains a same-device copy unless the operator establishes otherwise.

<a id="resource-references-linux-isolation-md"></a>

## Included resource: references/linux-isolation.md

# Optional Linux isolation and recovery

Use this route only for an authorized CPU task on an explicitly selected existing
Docker Linux/cgroup-v2 engine and existing compatible image. Ordinary research,
writing, retrieval and molecular tools require no Docker. The existing
[SSH controller](#resource-references-remote-execution-md) remains a separate trusted-task route.
The Linux client is not an automatic SSH launcher; to use it on another machine,
select and authorize that host, stage the approved package/data there, then run
this lifecycle on that host. Never migrate an uncertain job by resubmitting it.

## Boundary and device requirements

The selected engine, image and Linux kernel are trusted. Containers share a
kernel; this is not a claim of protection from a malicious Docker administrator,
image, host root or kernel escape. CPU only is accepted. GPU devices, rootless
engines, cgroup v1, remote Docker TCP/SSH contexts, Windows workers and schedulers
need separate implementation or acceptance. Supported limits must be observed;
missing controls cause failure rather than fallback to the trusted SSH mode.

Provide an existing Docker executable, explicit local context, engine identity
pin and existing immutable image ID. The tool never installs or starts a daemon,
pulls an image, accepts model terms or downloads a scientific model. Docker
Desktop and other clients may expose Linux engines, but only the documented
Linux CI configuration has actual enforcement acceptance. A profile or permission
flag records configuration; it does not authorize a host, task or data transfer.

The engine's cgroup-v2 memory and matching swap limit, CPU quota, PID cap and
monotonic wall deadline bound execution. CPU quota bounds **rate**, not aggregate
CPU seconds. Work, temporary files, control state and shared memory use separately
bounded tmpfs filesystems. Tmpfs also consumes container memory, so allow headroom
for the task and Python supervisor. Image layers, daemon metadata, approved staged
inputs and host-side collection are outside the workload scratch-space cap.

The root filesystem is read-only and network mode is `none`. Only loopback is
available. Safe staged `/etc/hosts`, `/etc/hostname` and `/etc/resolv.conf` are
also bound read-only; Docker's generated versions otherwise need separate care.
Inputs and supervisor mounts are read-only, with no writable host bind, daemon
socket, credentials, GPU or host namespace exposed. Image-declared volumes are
rejected; entrypoint, user, working directory, logging, healthcheck and restart
policy are explicitly set. Default Docker seccomp remains enabled.

A root PID1 supervisor retains only capabilities needed to change task UID/GID,
read task artifacts and terminate namespace descendants. The task runs as
UID/GID65534 with no supplementary groups or capabilities and no-new-privileges.
Its control receipt is root-protected. Namespace cleanup also catches descendants
that create a new process session; cancellation never targets a saved host PID.
Use a compatible image containing Python 3.10+ at the selected absolute path and
the task's existing dependencies. Programs requiring a writable root filesystem,
network, privileged operations or unsupported assets need another explicit plan.

## Profile and manifest

Store profiles and job state outside the installed skill. Example profile:

```json
{
  "schema_version": 1,
  "docker_executable": "/usr/bin/docker",
  "context": "default",
  "expected_engine_id": null,
  "container_python": "/usr/local/bin/python3"
}
```

Run inspection first, then copy its actual engine identity into
`expected_engine_id` before preparing work. `null` permits inspection only.
The context must use a local `unix://` or `npipe://` endpoint; a remote daemon
cannot interpret local bind paths safely under this contract. Paths containing
commas or newlines are refused; ordinary spaces are supported. Do not publish a
personal profile, host identity, credentials or approved scientific input data.

Use a fresh 32-character lowercase hexadecimal job ID and an existing local
`sha256:` followed by a 64-character immutable image ID, obtained by inspecting the selected
engine's existing image. Mutable image tags are refused. Example manifest:

```json
{
  "schema_version": 1,
  "job_id": "0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef",
  "image_id": "sha256:REPLACE_WITH_64_LOWERCASE_HEX_IMAGE_ID",
  "argv": ["/usr/local/bin/python3", "-c", "from pathlib import Path; Path('result.txt').write_text('control: zero')"],
  "inputs": [],
  "outputs": ["result.txt"],
  "required_device": "cpu",
  "resources": {
    "wall_seconds": 30,
    "retention_seconds": 120,
    "memory_bytes": 268435456,
    "cpu_millis": 1000,
    "pids": 64,
    "work_bytes": 33554432,
    "tmp_bytes": 8388608,
    "max_log_bytes": 65536,
    "max_output_bytes": 1048576
  }
}
```

The image placeholder is intentionally invalid until replaced. Each input record
has exactly `source`, `destination` and `sha256`; source is relative to the
manifest and destination relative to `/work`. Only explicitly named, normalized
relative regular files with verified hashes may be staged. Symlinks, hardlinks,
special files, traversal, configuration/credential paths, duplicate names and
file/directory overlaps are refused. Generated collection receipt/log names are
reserved. Inputs are capped at 8 MiB per file and 16 MiB total, with at most 32 files;
outputs 1–32 names and 16 MiB total maximum. No arbitrary archive extraction occurs.

All resource values are integers: wall 1–3600 seconds, retention 5–3600 seconds,
memory 64 MiB–8 GiB, CPU 100–16000 millis (1000 means one core of quota), PID limit 16–512,
work 1–256 MiB, temp 1–64 MiB, log 1 byte–1 MiB per stream, outputs 1 byte–16 MiB total.
Memory, work and temp byte budgets must be multiples of 1 MiB to avoid kernel
page rounding above the ceiling. Control tmpfs is 4 MiB and shared memory 1 MiB. Work and temp each
have 1024 inodes; control has 128, including directory entries. Choose sufficient work space for
inputs and memory for every tmpfs plus execution; these are explicit ceilings,
not a promise that an undersized task can finish.

## Lifecycle

Use new report paths for every invocation. The state directory and collection
destination must be new at preparation/publication; never overwrite another run.
Replace the placeholders below with the actual installed skill/project paths.

```sh
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/linux_tools.py inspect --profile PROJECT/linux.json --report PROJECT/inspection.json
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/linux_tools.py prepare --profile PROJECT/linux.json --manifest PROJECT/job.json --state PROJECT/job-state --allow-stage --report PROJECT/prepared.json
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/linux_tools.py submit --state PROJECT/job-state --allow-execute --report PROJECT/submitted.json
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/linux_tools.py status --state PROJECT/job-state --report PROJECT/status.json
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/linux_tools.py recover --state PROJECT/job-state --report PROJECT/recovered.json
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/linux_tools.py collect --state PROJECT/job-state --destination PROJECT/new-results --report PROJECT/collected.json
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/linux_tools.py cancel --state PROJECT/job-state --allow-cancel --report PROJECT/cancelled.json
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/linux_tools.py cleanup --state PROJECT/job-state --allow-remove --report PROJECT/removed.json
```

Preparation verifies inputs and stages the pinned controller without running the
task. Submission persists a permanent intent before creation. State binds the
selected engine, image, manifest, controller hash, random nonce and deterministic
container name; the full immutable container ID is recorded when recovered.
Stable OS locks release on client death while retaining the lock inode.

After an interrupted client, use `recover` on the **same state and engine**.
It inspects and validates the original object; it never creates, starts, restarts
or resubmits. If the create reply was lost, matching labels and the deterministic
name permit reconciling that original ID. A missing object after intent remains
uncertain. A created but unstarted object is not started by recovery. Changed
engine, object, nonce, image, manifest, controller or runtime configuration causes
refusal. Read-only Docker-exec queries can briefly fail during namespace cleanup;
retry status/recovery after inspecting the report, never submission.

Collection reads the root receipt and bounded declared files through `docker
exec`, validates names, sizes and hashes, then atomically publishes a new host
directory with `.collection.json`. It never uses `docker cp` for tmpfs. Failed,
cancelled and timed-out results retain honest partial/missing state. Exit 0 on a
stopped container is insufficient to call a scientific job successful. Cleanup
removes only the verified full container ID; it never deletes an arbitrary name.

## Retention and failure recovery

The supervisor survives host-client interruption and enforces the task deadline
without that client. After task completion it stays alive for the explicit finite
retention interval so outputs can be collected. **Collect before that interval
expires.** Temporary artifacts vanish when the container stops. Host reboot,
PID1 death, daemon failure or OOM can lose them earlier. Recovery reports lost or
unavailable artifacts rather than inventing a result or repeating the task.
Containers do not restart automatically. Stopped metadata may need explicit
cleanup. This release does not supply durable checkpointing or guarantee output
survival across host/controller death. A new job ID can duplicate scientific work;
resolve uncertainty about the original first.

Kernel enforcement evidence is distinct from scientific validation. A successful
control run proves only the stated lifecycle behavior for that configuration.
Predictions, simulations and embeddings still need suitable controls and task
specific validation. See the repository's phase-7 validation report for observed
platforms and unexecuted combinations.

## Primary interface references

The implementation is original MIT code. It uses Docker's documented CLI;
Docker, selected images and their components retain their own terms. See
[resource constraints](https://docs.docker.com/engine/containers/resource_constraints/),
[run options](https://docs.docker.com/reference/cli/docker/container/run/),
[tmpfs](https://docs.docker.com/engine/storage/tmpfs/),
[exec](https://docs.docker.com/reference/cli/docker/container/exec/) and
[copy limitations](https://docs.docker.com/reference/cli/docker/container/cp/).


## Durable application files (v0.12)

A newly prepared schema-2 job can explicitly declare a cooperative checkpoint
area. [Durable checkpoints](#resource-references-durable-checkpoints-md) capture its immutable published
files through read-only exec and commit them in a host-side store. Existing
schema-1 jobs remain unchanged. A checkpoint must be committed before the
container disappears; restoring it does not relaunch that job. The original
execution must be reconciled before preparing a permitted new continuation.
No writable host mount or automatic background watcher is added.

<a id="resource-references-literature-retrieval-md"></a>

## Included resource: references/literature-retrieval.md

# Bounded literature retrieval

`scripts/literature_tools.py` is an original MIT Python 3.10+ standard-library
adapter. Network requests occur only when one of its explicit commands runs.
Queries are sent to the selected public provider. Respect supplied-only boundaries
and do not submit private research details without authorization.

```sh
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/literature_tools.py search --provider crossref --query "literature review" --pages 2 --page-size 5 --output PROJECT/crossref.json
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/literature_tools.py search --provider europepmc --query "OPEN_ACCESS:Y AND malaria" --pages 2 --page-size 5 --output PROJECT/europepmc.json
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/literature_tools.py updates --doi 10.1371/journal.pcbi.1003149 --output PROJECT/updates.json
python PATH_TO_SKILL/scripts/literature_tools.py fulltext --pmcid PMC3715443 --text-output PROJECT/article.txt --output PROJECT/article.json
```

Choose a user-owned project directory. Existing outputs are preserved unless
`--force` is explicitly selected. Search bounds are one to five pages and one to
20 records per page. Crossref accepts optional `--filter` using its documented
syntax; Europe PMC filters belong in its explicit query syntax. Requests are
sequential, time out after ten seconds and cap each response at 10 MiB. A 429
stops the run and preserves Retry-After; it never causes unbounded retries.

Reports retain the actual query/filter, provider, date, page/cursor receipts,
raw record variants and identity provenance. Identical DOI records are grouped
while conflicting versions remain visible. Grouping canonical IDs is not evidence
that separate studies are independent or that different reports belong to a single
study. Reconcile evidence families separately. `exhausted` describes the provider
traversal only; a bounded search is never a comprehensive field review. Partial
failures retain the successful records and identify the failed request. Missing
records, failed requests and empty results do not establish scholarly absence.

The updates command retrieves an exact DOI record and reverse Crossref
`updates:DOI` notices. A notice's `update-to` points toward the affected article;
its own DOI is separate. Deposited relations and raw correction/retraction signals
are retained, not transformed into an assurance that the source is unretracted.
Inspect relevant notices and publisher corrections before using affected findings.

Search results provide metadata or returned abstracts. Full-text links and an
open-access flag do not mean the article has been retrieved or read. Full text is
requested only through Europe PMC's official OA XML endpoint using a valid PMCID.
The adapter verifies the returned article identity and nonempty body, retains
license/copyright and section locators, and writes a UTF-8 text artifact with its
SHA-256. Its XML-content hash identifies the retrieved revision; missing publisher
version information stays missing. External JATS DOCTYPE declarations are accepted
without fetching their resources; internal DTD/entity definitions are rejected.
Redirects outside fixed provider endpoints are blocked. No arbitrary publisher
URL, HTML scraping, API key, paid access or paywall bypass is provided.

Successful retrieval is access, not reading or semantic review. The report keeps
`read_or_reviewed: false` and `semantic_verification: not_performed`. To prepare a
source-support record, use a project-relative text path and `text_sha256`, the
observed PMCID as source identity, the retrieved `version_identifier` as a content
revision identifier (or an explicitly established publisher version), and the actual
passage/section locator. Review the passage against the claim separately; metadata
must not be upgraded into full-text support.

Full texts and abstracts retain article-specific rights. Unknown license means
unknown reuse rights, never MIT. Preserve attribution and inspect the article's
license before redistribution, figures or substantial reuse. This package contains
no downloaded papers. Service coverage is limited: Crossref spans registered
scholarly metadata, while Europe PMC primarily covers life-science sources. Neither
provides every discipline, article, correction, translation or inaccessible text.

Official interface and rights references: [Crossref cursor guidance](https://www.crossref.org/documentation/retrieve-metadata/rest-api/tips-for-using-the-crossref-rest-api/),
[Crossref access](https://www.crossref.org/documentation/retrieve-metadata/rest-api/access-and-authentication/),
[update filters](https://www.crossref.org/documentation/retrieve-metadata/rest-api/rest-api-filters/),
[Crossref metadata schema](https://github.com/CrossRef/rest-api-doc/blob/master/api_format.md),
[Europe PMC REST](https://europepmc.org/RestfulWebService),
[Europe PMC copyright](https://europepmc.org/Copyright).


For bounded anonymous OpenAlex discovery/reference IDs and official version-pinned
arXiv HTML, use the separate [deeper retrieval tool](#resource-references-deeper-retrieval-md). The
commands above retain their existing Crossref/Europe PMC contract; HTML handling
is specific to the new fixed arXiv route, not arbitrary publisher scraping.

<a id="resource-references-modes-md"></a>

## Included resource: references/modes.md

# Select the writing method

Read the sections relevant to the request; do not impose every section on every project. Confirm the intended artifact through the core intake before choosing its depth. Reporting checklists help describe work; they do not replace doing it. For a requested journal manuscript or publication-readiness assessment, also use [publication preparation](#resource-references-publication-workflow-md), including aim coverage and unresolved author/declaration facts. This route is not required for an ordinary exploratory brief.
